. For example, denote
h_{1}^{(1)}(x)\in H_{1}
as the projection of
\phi_{1}(x,z,m)
onto
\Lambda
. Then
h_{1}^{(1)}(x)
minimizes the following function

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big\{h_{1}^{(1)}(x)-\phi_{1}(X,Z,M)\big\}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{h_{1}^{(1)}(X)\}^{2}+\mathbb{E}\{\phi^{2}_{1}(X,Z,M)\}-2\mathbb{E}\big[h_{1}^{(1)}(X)\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\{\phi_{1}(X,Z,M)\mid X,Z\}}_{=0}\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{h_{1}^{(1)}(X)\}^{2}+\mathbb{E}\{\phi^{2}_{1}(X,Z,M)\},

among all functions in
H_{1}
, which is clearly satisfied if and only if
h_{1}^{(1)}(x)=0
, a.s. Thus
\phi_{1}(x,z,m)\perp H_{1}
. Observing that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big\{h_{2}(X,Z)\phi_{1}(X,Z,M)\big\}=\mathbb{E}\big[h_{2}(X,Z)\mathbb{E}\{\phi_{1}(X,Z,M)\mid X,Z\}\big]=0,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big\{h_{4}(X,Z,M,Y)\phi_{1}(X,Z,M)\big\}=\mathbb{E}\big[\phi_{1}(X,Z,M)\mathbb{E}\{h_{4}(X,Z,M,Y)\mid X,Z,M\}\big]=0,

for any
h_{2}(x,z)\in H_{2}
and
h_{4}(x,z,m,y)\in H_{4}
, we thus similarly have
\phi_{1}(x,z,m)\perp H_{2}\text{ and }\phi_{1}(x,z,m)\perp H_{4}
. So the projection of
\phi_{1}(x,z,m)
onto
\Lambda
is only the projection of
\phi_{1}(x,z,m)
onto
\Lambda_{2}
.

Now we obtain the projections of
\tilde{\xi}_{2},\tilde{\xi}_{2},\tilde{\xi}_{2}^{\prime},\tilde{\xi}_{3}^{\prime}
onto
\Lambda_{2}
one by one. For example, for
\tilde{\xi}_{2}
, this is equivalent to finding
\tilde{\ell}_{1}^{\top}s_{zm}(x\mid\beta^{*})\in\Lambda_{2}
solving the following optimization problem,

\displaystyle\arg\min_{\tilde{\ell}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\beta}}}\,\mathbb{E}\Big\{\tilde{\xi}_{2}(X,Z,M)-\tilde{\ell}_{1}^{\top}s_{ZM}(X\mid\beta^{*})\Big\}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\arg\min_{\tilde{\ell}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\beta}}}\,\tilde{\ell}_{1}^{\top}I_{\beta^{*}}\tilde{\ell}_{1}-2\tilde{\ell}_{1}^{\top}\mathbb{E}\big\{\tilde{\xi}_{2}(X,Z,M)s_{ZM}(X\mid\beta^{*})\big\}+\mathbb{E}\{\tilde{\xi}_{2}^{2}(X,Z,M)\},

which can be solved by standard quadratic programming
[
Boyd et al., 2004
]
. In particular, the solution is

\displaystyle\tilde{\ell}_{1,\diamond}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{\beta^{*}}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\big\{\tilde{\xi}_{2}(X,Z,M)s_{ZM}(X\mid\beta^{*})\big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{\beta^{*}}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\big\{{\xi}_{2}(X,Z,M)s_{ZM}(X\mid\beta^{*})\big\}-I_{\beta^{*}}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}({\xi}_{2}\mid X,Z)\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\{s_{ZM}(X\mid\beta^{*})\mid X,Z\}}_{=0}\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{\beta^{*}}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\big\{{\xi}_{2}(X,Z,M)s_{ZM}(X\mid\beta^{*})\big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{\beta^{*}}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\frac{1}{\pi_{Z}(X)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}1(M\leq m_{z})\{\mu_{1}(X,m_{1})-\mu_{0}(X,m_{0})\}k_{u^{*}}(u)

\displaystyle\cdot c_{\rho}^{(Z)}\big\{F_{1m_{1}}(X),F_{0m_{0}}(X)\big\}\Big\{\prod_{z\in\{0,1\}}f_{zm_{z}}(X)\Big\}du\cdot s_{ZM}(X\mid\beta^{*})\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{\beta^{*}}^{-1}\sum_{z=0,1}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}1(M\leq m_{z})\{\mu_{1}(X,m_{1})-\mu_{0}(X,m_{0})\}k_{u^{*}}(u)c_{\rho}^{(z)}\big\{F_{1m_{1}}(X),F_{0m_{0}}(X)\big\}

\displaystyle\cdot\Big\{\prod_{z\in\{0,1\}}f_{zm_{z}}(X)\Big\}du\cdot s_{ZM}(X\mid\beta^{*})\mid X,Z=z\Big]\Bigg]\quad\text{(LOTE)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{\beta^{*}}^{-1}\sum_{z=0,1}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{-\infty}^{m_{z}}\{\mu_{1}(X,m_{1})-\mu_{0}(X,m_{0})\}k_{u^{*}}(u)c_{\rho}^{(z)}\big\{F_{1m_{1}}(X),F_{0m_{0}}(X)\big\}

\displaystyle\cdot\Big\{\prod_{z\in\{0,1\}}f_{zm_{z}}(X)\Big\}du\cdot f^{\prime}_{zm}(X)\underbrace{f^{-1}_{zm}(X)f_{zm}(X)}_{=1}dmdu\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{\beta^{*}}^{-1}\sum_{z=0,1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\big\{\mu_{1}(X,m_{1})-\mu_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}c_{\rho}^{(z)}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X)\big\}{f}_{1m_{1}}(X){f}_{0m_{0}}(X)F^{\prime}_{zm_{z}}(X)du\Big].

Thus
\tilde{\ell}_{1,\diamond}^{\top}s_{zm}(x\mid\beta^{*})
is the projection of
\tilde{\xi}_{2}
onto
\Lambda_{1}
, which, combing with the previous discussions, is also the projection of
\tilde{\xi}_{2}
onto the whole
\Lambda
. With similar techniques, we have
\tilde{\ell}_{2,\diamond}^{\top}s_{zm}(x\mid\beta^{*})
is the projection of
\tilde{\xi}_{3}
onto
\Lambda
, such that

\displaystyle\tilde{\ell}_{2,\diamond}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{\beta^{*}}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\big\{{\xi}_{3}(X,Z,M)s_{ZM}(X\mid\beta^{*})\big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{z=0,1}I_{\beta^{*}}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\big\{\gamma_{2}^{(z)}(X,M)s_{ZM}(X\mid\beta^{*})\mid X,Z=z\big\}\Big].

When
z=1
, for the term on the right-hand side of above equation, we further have

\displaystyle I_{\beta^{*}}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\big\{\gamma_{2}^{(1)}(X,M)s_{ZM}(X\mid\beta^{*})\mid X,Z=1\big\}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{\beta^{*}}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\Big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}}\{\mu_{1}(X,M)-\mu_{0}(X,m_{0})\}k_{u^{*}}(M,m_{0})c_{\rho}\{F_{1M}(X),F_{0m_{0}}(X)\}f_{0m_{0}}(X)dm_{0}

\displaystyle\cdot f^{-1}(M\mid X,Z=1)f^{\prime}_{1M}(X)\mid X,Z=1\Big\}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{\beta^{*}}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\{\mu_{1}(X,m)-\mu_{0}(X,m_{0})\}k_{u^{*}}(m,m_{0})c_{\rho}\{F_{1m}(X),F_{0m_{0}}(X)\}f_{0m_{0}}(X)f^{\prime}_{1m}(X)dm_{0}dm\Big\}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{\beta^{*}}^{-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\big\{\mu_{1}(X,m_{1})-\mu_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}c_{\rho}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X)\big\}f_{0m_{0}}(X)f^{\prime}_{1m_{1}}(X)du\Big]

We can similarly deal with the
z=0
case and deduce,

\displaystyle\tilde{\ell}_{2,\diamond}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{\beta^{*}}^{-1}\sum_{z=0,1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\big\{\mu_{1}(X,m_{1})-\mu_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}c_{\rho}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X)\big\}f_{(1-z)m_{1-z}}(X)f^{\prime}_{zm_{z}}(X)du\Big].

Notice that
\tilde{\xi}_{2}^{\prime},\tilde{\xi}_{3}^{\prime}
have very similar structures as
\tilde{\xi}_{2},\tilde{\xi}_{3}
. Following the derivation strategy as above, we can show that
{\tilde{\ell}_{1,\diamond}}^{\prime\top}s_{zm}(x\mid\beta^{*}),{\tilde{\ell}_{2,\diamond}}^{\prime\top}s_{zm}(x\mid\beta^{*})
are the projections of
\tilde{\xi}_{2}^{\prime},\tilde{\xi}_{3}^{\prime}
, respectively, such that

\displaystyle{\tilde{\ell}_{1,\diamond}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{\beta^{*}}^{-1}\sum_{z=0,1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)c_{\rho}^{(z)}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X)\big\}{f}_{1m_{1}}(X){f}_{0m_{0}}(X)F^{\prime}_{zm_{z}}(X)du\Big],

\displaystyle{\tilde{\ell}_{2,\diamond}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{\beta^{*}}^{-1}\sum_{z=0,1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)c_{\rho}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X)\big\}f_{(1-z)m_{1-z}}(X)f^{\prime}_{zm_{z}}(X)du\Big].

Recalling that
\phi_{1}(x,z,m)
is a linear combiniation of
\tilde{\xi}_{2},\tilde{\xi}_{3},\tilde{\xi}_{2}^{\prime},\tilde{\xi}_{3}^{\prime}
, we have the projection of
\phi_{1}(x,z,m)
on
\Lambda
is the corresponding linear combination of
\tilde{\xi}_{2},\tilde{\xi}_{3},\tilde{\xi}_{2}^{\prime},\tilde{\xi}_{3}^{\prime}
’s projections on
\Lambda
. In particular, the projection of
\phi_{1}(x,z,m)
is

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{A}_{2}^{*}-\tau_{u^{*}}\mathcal{A}_{1}^{*}}{\mathbb{E}\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(X)\}}I_{\beta^{*}}^{-1}s_{zm}(x\mid\beta^{*}),

(S60)

where we recall that in Section
S1
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}^{*}_{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{z=0,1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)c_{\rho}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X)\big\}f_{(1-z)m_{1-z}}(X)\{f^{\prime}_{zm_{z}}(X)\}^{\top}du\Big]

\displaystyle+\sum_{z=0,1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\Big[c_{\rho}^{(z)}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X)\big\}{f}_{1m_{1}}(X){f}_{0m_{0}}(X)\big\{F^{\prime}_{zm_{z}}(X)\big\}^{\top}\Big]du\Big],

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}^{*}_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{z=0,1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\big\{\mu_{1}(X,m_{1})-\mu_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}c_{\rho}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X)\big\}f_{(1-z)m_{1-z}}(X)\{f^{\prime}_{zm_{z}}(X)\}^{\top}du\Big]

\displaystyle+\sum_{z=0,1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\big\{\mu_{1}(X,m_{1})-\mu_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}c_{\rho}^{(z)}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X)\big\}{f}_{1m_{1}}(X){f}_{0m_{0}}(X)\big\{F^{\prime}_{zm_{z}}(X)\big\}^{\top}du\Big].

Summarizing the projections of
\phi_{1}(\cdot)
–
\phi_{3}(\cdot)
on
\Lambda
defined in (
S57
) as above, and recalling (
), we conclude the projection of
\phi_{{\rm np}}(x,z,m,y)
on
\Lambda
, i.e., the semiparametric EIF of
\tau_{u^{*}}
when the parametric model of
\mathcal{M}_{{\rm ps}}
is known, is

\displaystyle\phi_{{\rm p}}(x,z,m,y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{A}^{*}_{2}-\tau_{u^{*}}\mathcal{A}^{*}_{1}}{\mathbb{E}\big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\big\}}I_{\beta^{*}}^{-1}s_{zm}(x\mid\beta^{*})+\frac{{\xi}_{1}(x,z,m)\{y-\mu_{z}(x,m)\}}{\mathbb{E}\big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\big\}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\mu_{1}(x,m_{1})-\mu_{0}(x,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(x)du-\tau_{u^{*}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(x)du}{\mathbb{E}\big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\big\}},

which shows
\phi_{{\rm p}}(x,z,m,y)=\phi_{{\rm kn}}(x,z,m,y)+\tilde{\phi}_{\mathrm{p}}(x,z,m)
by recalling (
7
) and (
S3
).

S8.3.4
Derivation of
\phi_{{\rm kn}}(x,z,m,y)
under Scenario (i)

Now we assume the distribution of
M
given
X,Z
is known and thus we have some model restriction. Since the model assumption becomes more restrictive, the
\phi(\cdot)
satisfying (
S38
) is not necessary to be an EIF but must be an influence function (IF) of
\tau_{u^{*}}
and the IF can be not unique
[
Bickel et al., 1993
]
. Therefore, we can still derive an IF of
\tau_{u^{*}}
in a similar yet simpler way as in Section
S8.3.2
. In particular, we find that
\phi(\cdot)=\phi_{{\rm kn}}(\cdot)
in (
10
) satisfying (
S38
) and thus
\phi_{{\rm kn}}(\cdot)
is an IF. Furthermore, based on the fundamental semiparametric statistics theory, we can verify that
\phi_{{\rm kn}}(\cdot)
is actually the EIF by showing that it belongs to the semiparametric tangent space and thereby finish our proof; See e.g.
Van der Vaart [2000,

§

25.3]
for the relevant semiparametric statistics theory used here.

We show that
\phi_{{\rm kn}}(\cdot)
is an IF following the proof routine in Section
S8.3.2
with some simplification. In particular, since
f_{zm}(x)
and
F_{zm}(x)
are all known, we do not need to parametrize
e_{u}(x)
as
e^{(\theta)}_{u}(x)
and take it into account when deriving the EIFs of

\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\mu_{1}(X,m_{1})-\mu_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(X)du\Big]

and
\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\mu_{1}(X,m_{1})-\mu_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(X)du\big]
in Section
S8.3.2
. Then we can show that the function

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(x)du-\mathbb{E}\Bigg\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(X)du\Bigg\},

which drops the terms
\tilde{\xi}^{\prime}_{2},\tilde{\xi}^{\prime}_{3}
in (
S49
), is the IF of

\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\mu_{1}(X,m_{1})-\mu_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(X)du\big]
under Scenario (i). Similarly, the function

\tilde{\xi}_{1}(x,z,m,y)+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\mu_{1}(x,m_{1})-\mu_{0}(x,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(x)du-\mathbb{E}\Bigg\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\mu_{1}(X,m_{1})-\mu_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(X)du\Bigg\},

which drops the terms
\tilde{\xi}_{2},\tilde{\xi}_{3}
in (
S54
), is the IF of

\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\mu_{1}(X,m_{1})-\mu_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(X)du\Big].

With simplified IFs of
\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\mu_{1}(X,m_{1})-\mu_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(X)du\big]
and
\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\mu_{1}(X,m_{1})-\mu_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}

k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(X)du\big]
as above and similar to (
S55
), we can use Lemma
S3
to show that
\phi(\cdot)=\phi_{{\rm kn}}(\cdot)
in (
10
) satisfying (
S38
) and thus
\phi_{{\rm kn}}(\cdot)
is an IF of
\tau_{u^{*}}
.

We now derive the semiparametric tangent space under Scenario (i). With known
\mathcal{M}_{{\rm ps}}
, the density function decomposition similar to (
) is now

\displaystyle f_{\theta}(V=v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f_{\theta}(X=x)f_{\theta}(Z=z\mid X=x)f(M=m\mid X=x,Z=z)f_{\theta}(Y=y\mid X=x,Z=z,M=m).

Thus score function of some arbitrary parametric submodel is now decomposed as

\displaystyle s_{\theta}(v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle s_{\theta}(x)+s_{\theta}(z\mid x)+s_{\theta}(y\mid x,z,m).

(S61)

By considering the mean-square closure of all functions
s_{\theta}(v)
over all possible submodels, the tangent space is now degenerated to,

\displaystyle\Lambda

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H_{1}\oplus H_{2}\oplus H_{4},

(S62)

where
\oplus
is the set sum and,

\displaystyle H_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{h(x)\mid\mathbb{E}\{h(X)\}=0\},

\displaystyle H_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{h(x,z)\mid\mathbb{E}\{h(X,Z)\mid X\}=0\},

\displaystyle H_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{h(x,z,m,y)\mid\mathbb{E}\{h(X,Z,M,Y)\mid X,Z,M\}=0\}.

First, observing that
\mathbb{E}[\xi_{1}(X,Z,M)\{Y-\mu_{Z}(X,M)\}]=\mathbb{E}[\xi_{1}(X,Z,M)\mathbb{E}\{Y-\mu_{Z}(X,M)\mid X,Z,M\}]=0
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{{\xi}_{1}(x,z,m)\{y-\mu_{z}(x,m)\}}{\mathbb{E}\big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(X)du\big\}}

\displaystyle\in

\displaystyle H_{4}.

Second, observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\mu_{1}(X,m_{1})-\mu_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(X)du-\tau_{u^{*}}\big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(X)du\big\}}{\mathbb{E}\big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(X)du\big\}}\Bigg]

(S63)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\mu_{1}(X,m_{1})-\mu_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(X)du\big]}{\mathbb{E}\big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(X)du\big\}}-\tau_{u^{*}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

by Theorem
2
. Recalling the form of
\phi_{{\rm kn}}(\cdot)
in
(
10
), thus for
\psi_{{\rm kn}}(x)
defined as follows, we have

\displaystyle\psi_{{\rm kn}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\mu_{1}(x,m_{1})-\mu_{0}(x,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(x)du-\tau_{u^{*}}\big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(x)du\big\}}{\mathbb{E}\big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(X)du\big\}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\phi_{{\rm kn}}(x,z,m,y)-\frac{{\xi}_{1}(x,z,m)\{y-\mu_{z}(x,m)\}}{\mathbb{E}\big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(X)du\big\}}\in H_{1}.

Summarizing above results, we conclude that IF
\phi_{{\rm kn}}(x,z,m,y)
belongs to the semiparametric tangent space, as

\displaystyle\phi_{{\rm kn}}(x,z,m,y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi_{{\rm kn}}(x)+\frac{{\xi}_{1}(x,z,m)\{y-\mu_{z}(x,m)\}}{\mathbb{E}\big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(X)du\big\}}

\displaystyle\in

\displaystyle H_{1}\oplus H_{4}\subseteq\Lambda.

This conclusion directly confirms that
\phi_{{\rm kn}}(\cdot)
is not only the IF, but also the EIF of
\tau_{u^{*}}
under the semiparametric scenario with known
\mathcal{M}_{{\rm ps}}
by the classic semiparametric statistics theory; See the discussions after Lemma 25.14 in
Van der Vaart [2000,

§

25.3]
.

S8.4
Proof of Theorem
4

With fixed
u^{*}
, we omit the footnote
u^{*}
in
\bar{\xi}_{1,u^{*}}(\cdot)
and directly write
\bar{\xi}_{1}(\cdot)
for simplicity. By definition, we further write,

\displaystyle\bar{{\xi}}_{1}(X,Z,M)\{Y-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\frac{\mathbb{I}(Z=1){\gamma_{1}}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)f(M\mid X,Z)}Y}_{\bar{\tilde{\xi}}^{(1)}_{1}(X,Z,M,Y)}-\underbrace{\frac{\mathbb{I}(Z=0){\gamma_{1}}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)f(M\mid X,Z)}Y}_{\bar{\tilde{\xi}}^{(0)}_{1}(X,Z,M,Y)}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\frac{\mathbb{I}(Z=1)\gamma_{1}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)f(M\mid X,Z)}\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)}_{\bar{E}_{\xi_{1}}^{(1)}(X,Z,M)}+\underbrace{\frac{\mathbb{I}(Z=0)\gamma_{1}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)f(M\mid X,Z)}\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)}_{\bar{E}_{\xi_{1}}^{(0)}(X,Z,M)}

We first consider the scenario when
\pi=\bar{\pi}
. Applying LOTE, one has

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big\{\bar{\tilde{\xi}}^{(1)}_{1}(X,Z,M,Y)\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg\{\frac{\mathbb{I}(Z=1){\gamma_{1}}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{\pi_{Z}(X)f(M\mid X,Z)}Y\Bigg\}

(S64)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}\Bigg\{\frac{\mathbb{I}(Z=1){\gamma_{1}}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{\pi_{Z}(X)f(M\mid X,Z)}Y\mid X\Bigg\}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\pi_{1}(X)\mathbb{E}\Bigg\{\frac{{\gamma_{1}}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{\pi_{1}(X)f(M\mid X,Z)}Y\mid X,Z=1\Bigg\}+\pi_{0}(X)\cdot 0\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}\Bigg\{\frac{{\gamma_{1}}^{(1)}(X,M)}{f(M\mid X,Z=1)}Y\mid X,Z=1\Bigg\}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}\Bigg\{\frac{{\gamma_{1}}^{(1)}(X,M)}{f(M\mid X,Z=1)}\mathbb{E}\big(Y\mid X,Z=1,M\big)\mid X,Z=1\Bigg\}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg\{\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{{\gamma_{1}}^{(1)}(M=m_{1},X)\mathbb{E}\big(Y\mid X,Z=1,M=m_{1}\big)}{f(M=m_{1}\mid X,Z=1)}f(M=m_{1}\mid X,Z=1)dm_{1}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg\{\int_{\mathbb{R}}{{\gamma_{1}}^{(1)}(M=m_{1},X)\mu_{1}(X,m_{1})}dm_{1}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}{k_{u^{*}}(m_{1},m_{0})e_{(m_{1},m_{0})}(X)\mu_{1}(X,m_{1})}du\Bigg\}.

Similarly, one has
\mathbb{E}\{\bar{\tilde{\xi}}^{(0)}_{1}(X,Z,M,Y)\}=\mathbb{E}\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}{k_{u^{*}}(m_{1},m_{0})e_{(m_{1},m_{0})}(X)\mu_{0}(X,m_{0})}du\}
. With same techniques as above, we can show

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big\{\bar{E}_{\xi_{1}}^{(1)}(X,Z,M)\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg\{\frac{\mathbb{I}(Z=1){\gamma_{1}}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{\pi_{Z}(X)f(M\mid X,Z)}\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}\Bigg\{\frac{{\gamma_{1}}^{(1)}(X,M)}{f(M\mid X,Z=1)}\bar{\mu}_{1}(X,M)\mid X,Z=1\Bigg\}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg\{\int_{\mathbb{R}}{{\gamma_{1}}^{(1)}(M=m_{1},X)}\bar{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})dm_{1}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}{k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(X)\bar{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})}du\Bigg\},

and, similarly
\mathbb{E}\big\{\bar{E}_{\xi_{1}}^{(0)}(X,Z,M)\big\}=\mathbb{E}\big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}{k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(X)\bar{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})}du\big\}
. Summarizing all results above,

\displaystyle\bar{\tau}_{u^{*}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}\big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\big\}}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\mu_{1}(X,m_{1})-\mu_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\Big]

(S65)

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\bar{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\bar{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\Big]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\bar{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\bar{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\Big]\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tau_{u^{*}},

where the last equality is from Theorem
8
and we recall (
7
).

Next we consider the scenario when
\bar{\mu}=\mu
. With same technique as (
S64
), we can show

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big\{\bar{\tilde{\xi}}^{(z)}_{1}(X,Z,M,Y)\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg\{\frac{\pi_{z}(X)}{\bar{\pi}_{z}(X)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}{k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(X)\mu_{z}(X,m_{z})}du\Bigg\}

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big\{\bar{E}_{\xi_{1}}^{(z)}(X,Z,M)\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg\{\frac{\pi_{z}(X)}{\bar{\pi}_{z}(X)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}{k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(X)\bar{\mu}_{z}(X,m_{z})}du\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg\{\frac{\pi_{z}(X)}{\bar{\pi}_{z}(X)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}{k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(X)\mu_{z}(X,m_{z})}du\Bigg\},

for both
z=0,1
. This implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big\{\bar{\tilde{\xi}}_{1}(X,Z,M,Y)\big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

(S66)

Finally since
\bar{\mu}=\mu
, by Theorem
2
we have

\displaystyle\bar{\tau}_{u^{*}}=\frac{0+\mathbb{E}\big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\mu_{1}(X,m_{1})-\mu_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\big]}{\mathbb{E}\big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\big\}}={\tau}_{u^{*}}.

Combining with Proposition
1
, we further have
\lim_{h\rightarrow 0}\bar{\tau}_{u^{*}}=\lim_{h\rightarrow 0}{\tau}_{u^{*}}=\tau^{*}_{u^{*}}

\Box

S8.5
Regularity assumptions for Theorem
5

We first discuss Assumption
4
in the main paper. Assumption
4
(i) allows either one of the models
\mathcal{M}_{{\rm tp}}
or
\mathcal{M}_{{\rm om}}
, to be incorrectly estimated, while consistency and limiting distribution results in Theorem
5
will still be valid. Assumption
4
(ii)–(iii) are standard which assume that when the sample size grows, nuisance function estimators are consistent to their limits under
\mathcal{L}_{\infty}
norm and specify their
\mathcal{L}_{2}
convergence rates to the truths. Assumption
4
(iv) restricts the functional space complexities of estimators
\hat{\pi}
,
\hat{\mu}
and their limits, which is a usual minimal condition for the use of empirical process techniques in theoretical analysis; See e.g.,
Kennedy [2016]
,
Westling et al. [2020]
.

Now we list other regularity conditions used for Theorem
5
as follows.

Assumption S8

There exists some
\epsilon>0
such that
\pi_{z}(X),\bar{\pi}_{z}(X)\in(\epsilon,1/\epsilon)
, uniformly for all
X\in\mathbb{R}
and
z=0,1
.

Assumption S9

For some universal constant
C>0
, we have

\max\Bigg\{\sup_{h>0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)du,\,\sup_{z\in\{0,1\},h>0,m_{z}\in\mathbb{R}}h\int_{\mathbb{R}}k_{u^{*}}(u)dm_{1-z},\,\sup_{h>0}h^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k^{2}_{u^{*}}(u)du\Bigg\}\leq C.

Assumption S10

(1)
\mathbb{E}\{e_{U}(X)\}<+\infty
; (2)
c(u,v)
and all its first and second-order partial derivatives are bounded away from
+\infty
, uniformly over
(u,v)\in[-\infty,\infty]\times[-\infty,\infty]
; (3)
c^{(10)}(u,v)=c^{(01)}(u,v)
.

Assumption S11

\sup_{u\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}e_{u}=\sup_{(m_{1},m_{0})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}f(M_{1}=m_{1},M_{0}=m_{0})<C_{M}
for a universal constant
C_{M}>0
.

Assumption S12

\mathbb{Y}
is compact and thus
\sup_{x,m\in\mathbb{R}\atop z\in\{0,1\}}\text{Var}(Y\mid X=x,Z=z,M=m)<+\infty
.

Assumption S13

For any
g_{zm_{z}}(\cdot\mid\beta)\in\big\{f_{zm_{z}}(\cdot\mid\beta),F_{zm_{z}}(\cdot\mid\beta)\big\}
and any
\tilde{\beta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\beta}}
, denote

g^{\prime}_{zm_{z}}(\cdot\mid\tilde{\beta})=\frac{\partial}{\partial\beta}g_{zm_{z}}(\cdot\mid\beta)\ \Big|_{\beta=\tilde{\beta}}
and
g^{\prime\prime}_{zm_{z}}(\cdot\mid\tilde{\beta})=\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\beta\partial\beta^{\top}}g_{zm_{z}}(\cdot\mid\beta)\ \Big|_{\beta=\tilde{\beta}}.
There exist constants
c_{0},C_{0},\delta_{0}>0
such that,
\inf_{m_{z},x\in\mathbb{R}\atop z\in\{0,1\}}f_{zm_{z}}(x\mid\beta^{*})\geq c_{0}
and

\displaystyle\max

\displaystyle\Big\{\sup_{{m_{z},x\in\mathbb{R}\atop z\in\{0,1\}}}\big|g_{zm_{z}}(x\mid\beta^{*})\big|,\sup_{{m_{z},x\in\mathbb{R}\atop z\in\{0,1\}}}\big\|g^{\prime}_{zm_{z}}(x\mid\beta^{*})\big\|,\sup_{m_{z},x\in\mathbb{R}\atop{z\in\{0,1\}\atop{\|\tilde{\beta}-\beta^{*}\|\leq\delta_{0}}}}\big\|g^{\prime\prime}_{zm_{z}}(x\mid\tilde{\beta})\big\|_{2}\Big\}\leq C_{0}.

Assumption
S8
is the standard overlap condition
[
Rosenbaum and Rubin, 1983
]
. Assumptions
S9
–
S10
can be satisfied by most of the commonly-used kernel functions and copula functions
[
Jaworski et al., 2010
,
Fan, 1993
,
Wasserman, 2006
]
. Assumptions
S11
–
S12
are standard density and moment conditions for the data distribution
[
Chen and Christensen, 2015
,
Luedtke and Van Der Laan, 2016
,
Chu et al., 2023
]
. The condition of a compact
\mathbb{Y}
in Assumption
S12
is only for controling the empirical process term (see Section
S9
), and can be further relaxed if one exploits the standard sample splitting technique. Assumption
S13
is a standard regularity condition to bound the first and second-order derivatives of some parametrized likelihood
[
Van der Vaart, 2000
,
Fan and Peng, 2004
]
.

S8.6
Proof of Theorem
5

Although
h
is fixed for Loc.PCE, to further enable our nonparametric theoretical analysis of Cont.PCE in Section
S8.7
, we consider a more general setting in the proof such that we allow
h\rightarrow 0
. Our results in Theorem
5
can be easily obtained by setting
h
as fixed in our general results. For the ease of exposition, in the following, we will omit the footnote
u^{*}
in
\hat{\xi}_{1,u^{*}}(\cdot)
,
\bar{\xi}_{1,u^{*}}(\cdot)
, etc, and directly write
\hat{\xi}_{1}(\cdot)
,
\bar{\xi}_{1}(\cdot)
, etc. We will also occasionally omit random variables in
\hat{\xi}_{1}(X,Z,M)
and directly write
\hat{\xi}_{1}
for simplicity. On the other hand, for better illustration, we expand the notation of
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
through its definition in (
7
) during the proof, and only use
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
when wrapping up results.

We first define

\displaystyle\Delta_{1,n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})\Big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\Big\}+\mathbb{P}_{n}\Big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\hat{e}_{u}(X)du-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\Big\},

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\Big[\hat{\xi}_{1}(X,Z,M)\{Y-\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\hat{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\hat{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u)\hat{e}_{u}(X)du\Big]

\displaystyle-\mathbb{P}_{n}\Big[\bar{\xi}_{1}(X,Z,M)\{Y-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\bar{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\bar{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\Big].

Then by Taylor expansion, we have for some
\delta_{n}\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}_{u^{*}}-\tau_{u^{*}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\tau_{u^{*}}+\frac{\mathbb{P}_{n}\big[\hat{{\xi}}_{1}\{Y-\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\hat{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\hat{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u)\hat{e}_{u}(X)du\big]}{\mathbb{P}_{n}\big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\hat{e}_{u}(X)du\big\}}

(S67)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\tau_{u^{*}}+\frac{\mathbb{P}_{n}\big[\bar{{\xi}}_{1}\{Y-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\bar{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\bar{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\big]+\Delta_{2,n}}{\mathbb{E}\big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\big\}+\Delta_{1,n}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\tau_{u^{*}}+\frac{\mathbb{P}_{n}\big[\bar{{\xi}}_{1}\{Y-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\bar{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\bar{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\big]+\Delta_{2,n}}{\mathbb{E}\big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\big\}}

\displaystyle-\frac{\mathbb{P}_{n}\big[\bar{{\xi}}_{1}\{Y-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\bar{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\bar{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\big]+\Delta_{2,n}}{\big[\mathbb{E}\big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\big\}\big]^{2}}\Delta_{1,n}

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathbb{P}_{n}\big[\bar{{\xi}}_{1}\{Y-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\bar{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\bar{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\big]+\Delta_{2,n}}{\big[\mathbb{E}\big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\big\}+\delta_{n}\Delta_{1,n}\big]^{3}}\Delta^{2}_{1,n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})\big[\bar{{\xi}}_{1}\{Y-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\bar{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\bar{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\big]+\Delta_{2,n}}{\mathbb{E}\big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\big\}}

\displaystyle-\frac{\mathbb{P}\big[\bar{{\xi}}_{1}\{Y-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\bar{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\bar{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\big]}{\big[\mathbb{E}\big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\big\}\big]^{2}}\Delta_{1,n}

\displaystyle-\frac{(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})\big[\bar{{\xi}}_{1}\{Y-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\bar{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\bar{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\big]+\Delta_{2,n}}{\big[\mathbb{E}\big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\big\}\big]^{2}}\Delta_{1,n}

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathbb{P}_{n}\big[\bar{{\xi}}_{1}\{Y-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\bar{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\bar{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\big]+\Delta_{2,n}}{\big[\mathbb{P}\big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\big\}+\delta_{n}\Delta_{1,n}\big]^{3}}\Delta^{2}_{1,n}

where the last equality is by the double robustness introduced in Theorem
4
. Next, in Sections
S8.6.1
and
S8.6.2
, we bound the relative error terms above, respectively.

S8.6.1
Bounding
\Delta_{1,n}

For simplicity, we denote

\Delta_{1,n}^{(1)}=(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})\Big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\Big\}\quad\text{and}\quad\Delta_{1,n}^{(2)}=\mathbb{P}_{n}\Big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\hat{e}_{u}(X)du-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\Big\}.

Thus we have
\Delta_{1,n}=\Delta_{1,n}^{(1)}+\Delta_{1,n}^{(2)}
.

Notice that,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\Big\}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k^{2}_{u^{*}}(u)du\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}{e}^{2}_{u}(X)du\Big\}\quad\text{(Cauchy--Schwartz inequality)}

(S68)

\displaystyle\precsim

\displaystyle h^{-2}\cdot\mathbb{P}\Big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}{e}^{2}_{u}(X)du\Big\}\quad\text{(Assumption \ref{am:kernel})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h^{-2}\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f^{2}(U=u\mid X=x)\cdot f(X=x)dxdu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h^{-2}\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f(U=u\mid X=x)\cdot f(X=x,U=u)dxdu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h^{-2}\cdot\mathbb{E}\{e_{U}(X)\}\precsim h^{-2}.\quad(\text{Assumption \ref{am:eux}}).

Then by a standard argument based on the Chebyshev’s inequality, we conclude

\displaystyle\mathrm{pr}\Bigg[\Big|\mathbb{P}_{n}\Big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\Big\}-\mathbb{P}\Big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\Big\}\Big|\geq kh^{-1}n^{-1/2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\precsim

\displaystyle k^{-2},

for any
k>0
, which implies that,

\displaystyle\Delta_{1,n}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})\Big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\Big\}

(S69)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2}).

On the other hand, by Taylor expansion one has

\displaystyle\Delta_{1,n}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\Big[c_{\rho}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\hat{\beta}),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\hat{\beta})\big\}{f}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\hat{\beta}){f}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\hat{\beta})-e_{u}(X)\Big]du\Bigg]

(S70)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{P}_{n}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\Big[c_{\rho}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\beta^{*}),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\big\}{f}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\hat{\beta}){f}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\hat{\beta})-e_{u}(X)\Big]du\Big]}_{\Delta_{1,n}^{(2,1)}}

(S71)

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sum_{z=0,1}\mathbb{P}_{n}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\cdot\Big[c_{\rho}^{(z)}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\beta^{*}),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\big\}{f}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\hat{\beta}){f}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\hat{\beta})\big\{F_{zm_{z}}(X\mid\hat{\beta})-F_{zm_{z}}(X\mid{\beta^{*}})\big\}\Big]du\Big]}_{\Delta_{1,n}^{(2,2)}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}\sum_{z=0,1}\mathbb{P}_{n}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\Big[c_{\rho}^{(zz)}\big\{\xi_{1,n},\xi_{0,n}\big\}{f}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\hat{\beta}){f}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\hat{\beta})\big\{F_{zm_{z}}(X\mid\hat{\beta})-F_{zm_{z}}(X\mid{\beta^{*}})\big\}^{2}\Big]du\Big]}_{\Delta_{1,n}^{(2,3)}}

\displaystyle\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{$\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{P}_{n}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\Big[c_{\rho}^{(10)}\big\{\xi_{1,n},\xi_{0,n}\big\}{f}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\hat{\beta}){f}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\hat{\beta})\prod_{z=0,1}\big\{F_{zm_{z}}(X\mid\hat{\beta})-F_{zm_{z}}(X\mid{\beta^{*}})\big\}\Big]du\Big]}_{\Delta_{1,n}^{(2,4)}}$}},

where
\xi_{z,n}=\delta_{z,n}{F}_{zm_{z}}(X\mid\beta^{*})+(1-\delta_{z,n}){F}_{zm_{z}}(X\mid\hat{\beta})
, for some
\delta_{z,n}\in[0,1]
and
z=0,1
. We recall that
c^{(1)}(a,b)=\frac{\partial c(a,b)}{\partial a}
,
c^{(11)}(a,b)=\frac{\partial^{2}c(a,b)}{\partial^{2}a}
and analogously for other notations, and in (
S71
) we use the symmetric property of the copula function under Assumption
S10
. With straightforward algebra, we further have

\displaystyle{f}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\hat{\beta}){f}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\hat{\beta})-{f}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid{\beta^{*}}){f}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid{\beta^{*}})

(S72)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big\{f_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\hat{\beta})-{f}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\big\}\big\{{f}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\hat{\beta})-{f}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\big\}

\displaystyle+\big\{f_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\hat{\beta})-{f}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\big\}{f}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\beta^{*})

\displaystyle+\big\{f_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\hat{\beta})-{f}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\big\}{f}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\beta^{*}),

meanwhile by Taylor expansion we actually have for
z=0,1
,

\displaystyle f_{zm_{z}}(X\mid\hat{\beta})-f_{zm_{z}}(X\mid\beta^{*})=\{f^{\prime}_{zm_{z}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\}^{\top}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})+(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})^{\top}f^{\prime\prime}_{zm_{z}}(X\mid\tilde{\beta}_{X,m_{z}})(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}),

(S73)

where
\tilde{\beta}_{X,m_{z}}=\delta_{X,m_{z}}\hat{\beta}+(1-\delta_{X,m_{z}})\beta^{*}
for some
\delta_{X,m_{z}}\in[0,1]
. Combining (
S72
) and (
S73
), the first term on the right-hand side of (
S71
) equals to

\displaystyle\Delta_{1,n}^{(2,1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)c_{\rho}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\beta^{*}),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\big\}

(S74)

\displaystyle\cdot\big\{{f}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\hat{\beta}){f}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\hat{\beta})-{f}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid{\beta^{*}}){f}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid{\beta^{*}})\big\}du\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)c_{\rho}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\beta^{*}),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\big\}

\displaystyle\cdot\Big[(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})^{\top}f^{\prime}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\{f^{\prime}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\}^{\top}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})

\displaystyle+(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})^{\top}f^{\prime}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\cdot(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})^{\top}f^{\prime\prime}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\tilde{\beta}_{X,m_{0}})(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})

\displaystyle+(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})^{\top}f^{\prime}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\cdot(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})^{\top}f^{\prime\prime}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\tilde{\beta}_{X,m_{1}})(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})

\displaystyle+(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})^{\top}f^{\prime\prime}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\tilde{\beta}_{X,m_{0}})(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})\cdot(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})^{\top}f^{\prime\prime}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\tilde{\beta}_{X,m_{1}})(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})\Big]du\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\sum_{z=0,1}\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)c_{\rho}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\beta^{*}),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\big\}

\displaystyle\cdot\Big[f_{(1-z)m_{1-z}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\{f^{\prime}_{zm_{z}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\}^{\top}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})

\displaystyle+f_{(1-z)m_{1-z}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\cdot(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})^{\top}f^{\prime\prime}_{zm_{z}}(X\mid\tilde{\beta}_{X,m_{z}})(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})\Big]du\Bigg].

Since
\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})
under a parametric model of
\mathcal{M}_{{\rm ps}}
, we have with probability approaching 1 (wpa1),
\|\tilde{\beta}_{x,m_{z}}-\beta^{*}\|\leq\delta_{0}
uniformly for all
x,m_{z}\in\mathbb{R}
when
n
is sufficiently large. Denote
\|\cdot\|_{2}
the matrix spectral norm. Then by Assumption
S13
, we have both
\sup_{m_{z},x\in\mathbb{R}}\|f^{\prime\prime}_{zm_{z}}(x\mid\tilde{\beta}_{x,m_{z}})\|_{2}
and
\sup_{m_{z},x\in\mathbb{R}}\|f^{\prime}_{zm_{z}}(x\mid\tilde{\beta}_{x,m_{z}})\|
are
\mathcal{O}(1)
for both
z=0,1
. By the bounds of
k_{u^{*}}(\cdot,\cdot)
and
c(\cdot,\cdot)
given in Assumptions
S9
and
S10
, we can bound the first and second terms in (
S74
) as following,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)c_{\rho}\big\{F_{1m_{1}}(X),F_{0m_{0}}(X)\big\}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})^{\top}f^{\prime}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\{f^{\prime}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\}^{\top}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})du\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\Big|(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})^{\top}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)c_{\rho}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(x),{F}_{0m_{0}}(x)\big\}f^{\prime}_{1m_{1}}(x\mid\beta^{*})\{f^{\prime}_{0m_{0}}(x\mid\beta^{*})\}^{\top}du\cdot(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\|^{2}\cdot\sup_{(u,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}|c(u,v)|\cdot\prod_{z=1,0}\sup_{m_{z}\in\mathbb{R},x\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{X}}}\|f_{zm_{z}}^{\prime}(x\mid\beta^{*})\|\underbrace{\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k^{2}_{u^{*}}(u)du}}_{\tiny{\mathcal{O}(h^{-1})\text{ by Assumption\penalty\ \ref{am:kernel}}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1})

and similarly,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)c_{\rho}\big\{F_{1m_{1}}(X),F_{0m_{0}}(X)\big\}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})^{\top}f^{\prime}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\cdot(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})^{\top}f^{\prime\prime}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\tilde{\beta}_{X,m_{0}})(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})du\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\|^{3}\sup_{(u,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}|c(u,v)|\sup_{m_{1}\in\mathbb{R},x\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{X}}}\|f_{1m_{1}}^{\prime}(x\mid\beta^{*})\|\sup_{m_{0}\in\mathbb{R},x\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{X}}}\|f^{\prime\prime}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\tilde{\beta}_{X,m_{0}})\|_{2}\cdot\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k^{2}_{u^{*}}(u)du}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-3/2}).

With similar argument, we can finally show the first term in (
S71
) is

\displaystyle\Delta_{1,n}^{(2,1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{z=0,1}\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)c_{\rho}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X)\big\}f_{(1-z)m_{1-z}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\{f^{\prime}_{zm_{z}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\}^{\top}du\Bigg](\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})

\displaystyle+o_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{z=0,1}\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)c_{\rho}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X)\big\}f_{(1-z)m_{1-z}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\{f^{\prime}_{zm_{z}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\}^{\top}du\Bigg](\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})

\displaystyle+o_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2});

Here in the last equality, by Chebyshev’s inequality, we can easily show that,

\displaystyle(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)c_{\rho}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X)\big\}f_{(1-z)m_{1-z}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\{f^{\prime}_{zm_{z}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\}^{\top}du\Bigg]\cdot(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2})\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1}).

Note here
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)c_{\rho}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X)\big\}f_{(1-z)m_{1-z}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\{f^{\prime}_{zm_{z}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\}^{\top}du
is a random variable with finite variance under Assumptions
S9
–
S10
. With similar argument, we can finally show the second term on the right-hand side of (
S71
) is

\displaystyle\Delta_{1,n}^{(2,2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{z=0,1}\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\Big[c_{\rho}^{(z)}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X)\big\}{f}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid{\beta}^{*}){f}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid{\beta}^{*})\big\{F^{\prime}_{zm_{z}}(X\mid{\beta}^{*})\big\}^{\top}\Big]du\Bigg](\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})

(S75)

\displaystyle+o_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2}),

and
\Delta_{1,n}^{(2,3)}
–
\Delta_{1,n}^{(2,4)}
on the right-hand side of (
S71
) are both
o_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2})
. Summarizing the results above, we finally conclude,

\displaystyle\Delta_{1,n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})\Big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\Big\}

(S76)

\displaystyle+\sum_{z=0,1}\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)c_{\rho}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X)\big\}f_{(1-z)m_{1-z}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\{f^{\prime}_{zm_{z}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\}^{\top}du\Bigg](\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})

\displaystyle+\sum_{z=0,1}\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\Big[c_{\rho}^{(z)}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X)\big\}{f}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid{\beta}^{*}){f}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid{\beta}^{*})\big\{F^{\prime}_{zm_{z}}(X\mid{\beta}^{*})\big\}^{\top}\Big]du\Bigg](\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})

\displaystyle+o_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{{\Gamma}_{1,n}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2})}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2})=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2}),

where the last equality follows from (
S69
),
\|\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\|=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})
, and the facts that for
z\in\{0,1\}
,

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathbb{P}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)c_{\rho}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X)\big\}f_{(1-z)m_{1-z}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\{f^{\prime}_{zm_{z}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\}^{\top}du\Big]\Big\|

(S77)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{(u,v)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}c_{\rho}(u,v)\sup_{m_{1-z}\in\mathbb{R},\,x\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{X}}}\Big|f_{(1-z)m_{1-z}}(x\mid\beta^{*})\Big|\sup_{m_{z}\in\mathbb{R}\,x\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{X}}}\big\|f^{\prime}_{zm_{z}}(x\mid\beta^{*})\big\|\cdot\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k^{2}_{u^{*}}(u)du}

\displaystyle\precsim

\displaystyle h^{-1}.

Under Assumptions
S10
and
S13
, we similarly have,

\Big\|\mathbb{P}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\big[c_{\rho}^{(z)}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X)\big\}{f}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid{\beta}^{*}){f}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid{\beta}^{*})\big\{F^{\prime}_{zm_{z}}(X\mid{\beta}^{*})\big\}^{\top}\big]du\Big]\Big\|\precsim h^{-1}.

S8.6.2
Bounding
\Delta_{2,n}

We now bound
\Delta_{2,n}
. Decompose
\Delta_{2,n}
as

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,n}

(S78)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\Big[\big\{\hat{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\hat{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}\big\{\hat{e}_{u}(X)-{e}_{u}(X)\big\}\Big]du\Bigg]$}}}_{\Delta_{2,n}^{(1)}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\scalebox{0.8}{\mbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\Big[\hat{\xi}_{1}\{Y-\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\}-\bar{\xi}_{1}\{Y-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\}\Big]+\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\big[\{\hat{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\hat{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\}-\{\bar{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\bar{\mu}_{0m_{0}}(X)\}\big]{e}_{u}(X)du\Bigg]$}}}_{\Delta_{2,n}^{(2)}}.

We bound the two terms above respectively. For
\Delta_{2,n}^{(1)}
, we further decompose it as

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,n}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\Big[\big\{\bar{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\bar{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}\{\hat{e}_{u}(X)-{e}_{u}(X)\}\Big]du\Bigg]}_{\Delta_{2,n}^{(1,1)}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\big[\{\hat{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\hat{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\}-\{\bar{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\bar{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\}\big]\{\hat{e}_{u}(X)-{e}_{u}(X)\}du\Bigg]}_{\Delta_{2,n}^{(1,2)}}.

A similar argument to (
S70
)–(
S75
) that handles
\mathbb{P}_{n}\big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\big\{\hat{e}_{u}(X)-e_{u}(X)\big\}du\big]
can similarly show that

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,n}^{(1,1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{z=0,1}\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\big\{\bar{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\bar{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}c_{\rho}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\beta^{*}),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\big\}f_{(1-z)m_{1-z}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\{f^{\prime}_{zm_{z}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\}^{\top}du\Bigg]

\displaystyle(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})

\displaystyle+\sum_{z=0,1}\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\big\{\bar{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\bar{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}c_{\rho}^{(a)}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\beta^{*}),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\big\}

\displaystyle{f}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid{\beta}^{*}){f}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid{\beta}^{*})\big\{F^{\prime}_{zm_{z}}(X\mid{\beta}^{*})\big\}^{\top}du\Bigg]

\displaystyle(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})

\displaystyle+o_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{{\Gamma}_{2,n}^{(1)}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2})}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2}),

under Assumptions
4
and
S9
. Note
\Delta_{2,n}^{(1,2)}
is built by
\mathbb{P}_{n}\big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\big\{\hat{e}_{u}(X)-e_{u}(X)\big\}du\big]
with an additional factor
\{\hat{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\hat{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\}-\{\bar{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\bar{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\}
in the integrand. Thus we can similarly use the Taylor expansion technique shown in (
S70
)–(
S75
) to deduce

\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\big\{\hat{e}_{u}(X)-e_{u}(X)\big\}du\Bigg]=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2}),

with a uniform bound such that

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{X}}}\Big|\{\hat{\mu}_{1}(x,m_{1})-\hat{\mu}_{0}(x,m_{0})\}-\{\bar{\mu}_{1}(x,m_{1})-\bar{\mu}_{0}(x,m_{0})\}\Big|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

under Assumption
4
, thus we have
\Delta_{2,n}^{(1,2)}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2})
. In summary, one has

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,n}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\Gamma}_{2,n}^{(1)}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2}).

For
\Delta_{2,n}^{(2)}
, we decompose

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,n}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}\hat{\gamma}_{1}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X){f}_{ZM}(X\mid\hat{\beta})}\big\{Y-\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\Bigg]-\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}{\gamma}_{1}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X){f}_{ZM}(X\mid{\beta}^{*})}\big\{Y-\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\Bigg]

(S79)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}{\gamma}_{1}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X){f}_{ZM}(X\mid{\beta}^{*})}\big\{Y-\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\Bigg]-\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}{\gamma}_{1}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X){f}_{ZM}(X\mid{\beta}^{*})}\big\{Y-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\big[\{\hat{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\hat{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\}-\{\bar{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\bar{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\}\big]{e}_{u}(X)du\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\Big\{\frac{\hat{\gamma}_{1}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{{f}_{ZM}(X\mid\hat{\beta})}-\frac{{\gamma}_{1}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{{f}_{ZM}(X\mid\beta^{*})}\Big\}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\Big[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}-\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\Big]\Big\{\frac{\hat{\gamma}_{1}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{{f}_{ZM}(X\mid\hat{\beta})}-\frac{{\gamma}_{1}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{{f}_{ZM}(X\mid\beta^{*})}\Big\}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}{\gamma}_{1}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X){f}_{ZM}(X\mid{\beta}^{*})}\big\{Y-\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\{\hat{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\hat{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\}{e}_{u}(X)du\Bigg]

\displaystyle-\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}{\gamma}_{1}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X){f}_{ZM}(X\mid{\beta}^{*})}\big\{Y-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\{\bar{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\bar{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\}{e}_{u}(X)du\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\Big\{\frac{\hat{\gamma}_{1}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{{f}_{ZM}(X\mid\hat{\beta})}-\frac{{\gamma}_{1}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{{f}_{ZM}(X\mid\beta^{*})}\Big\}\Bigg]}_{\Delta_{2,n}^{(2,1)}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\Big[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}-\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\Big]\Big\{\frac{\hat{\gamma}_{1}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{{f}_{ZM}(X\mid\hat{\beta})}-\frac{{\gamma}_{1}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{{f}_{ZM}(X\mid\beta^{*})}\Big\}\Bigg]}_{\Delta_{2,n}^{(2,2)}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\Big[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}-\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\Big]\Big\{\frac{\hat{\gamma}_{1}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{{f}_{ZM}(X\mid\hat{\beta})}-\frac{{\gamma}_{1}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{{f}_{ZM}(X\mid\beta^{*})}\Big\}\Bigg]}_{\Delta_{2,n}^{(2,3)}}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}{\gamma}_{1}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X){f}_{ZM}(X\mid{\beta}^{*})}\big\{Y-\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\big[\{\hat{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\hat{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\}{e}_{u}(X)\big]du\Bigg]

\displaystyle-\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}{\gamma}_{1}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X){f}_{ZM}(X\mid{\beta}^{*})}\big\{Y-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\big[\{\bar{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\bar{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\}{e}_{u}(X)\big]du\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,n}^{(2,1)}+\Delta_{2,n}^{(2,2)}+\Delta_{2,n}^{(2,3)}+\mathbb{P}_{n}\{h(V\mid\hat{\pi},\hat{\mu})-h(V\mid\bar{\pi},\bar{\mu})\},

where we denote

\displaystyle h(V\mid{\pi},{\mu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}\gamma_{1}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{\pi_{Z}(X)f_{ZM}(X)}\{Y-\mu_{Z}(X,M)\}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\{\mu_{1}(X,m_{1})-\mu_{0}(X,m_{0})\}e_{u}(X)du.

First, we study
\Delta_{2,n}^{(2,1)}
. By Taylor expansion, one has

\displaystyle\frac{\hat{\gamma}_{1}^{(1)}(X,M)}{{f}_{1M}(X\mid\hat{\beta})}-\frac{{\gamma}_{1}^{(1)}(X,M)}{{f}_{1M}(X\mid\beta^{*})}

(S81)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}k_{u^{*}}(M,m_{0})c_{\rho}\big\{F_{1M}(X\mid\hat{\beta}),F_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\hat{\beta})\big\}f_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\hat{\beta})dm_{0}

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}}k_{u^{*}}(M,m_{0})c_{\rho}\big\{F_{1M}(X\mid\beta^{*}),F_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\big\}f_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\beta^{*})dm_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[\underbrace{\{F^{\prime}_{1M}(X\mid\beta^{*})\}^{\top}\int_{\mathbb{R}}k_{u^{*}}(M,m_{0})c_{\rho}^{(1)}\big\{F_{1M}(X),F_{0X}(m_{0})\big\}f_{0m_{0}}(X)dm_{0}}_{\delta^{(1)}_{X,1,M}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\int_{\mathbb{R}}k_{u^{*}}(M,m_{0})c_{\rho}^{(0)}\big\{F_{1M}(X),F_{0m_{0}}(X)\big\}\{F^{\prime}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\}^{\top}f_{0m_{0}}(X)dm_{0}}_{\delta^{(2)}_{X,1,M}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\int_{\mathbb{R}}k_{u^{*}}(M,m_{0})c_{\rho}\big\{F_{1M}(X),F_{0m_{0}}(X)\big\}\{f^{\prime}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\}^{\top}dm_{0}}_{\delta^{(3)}_{X,1,M}}\Big]\cdot(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})

\displaystyle+(\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}-\beta^{*})^{\top}I_{X,1,M}(\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}-\beta^{*})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(\delta^{(1)}_{X,1,M}+\delta^{(2)}_{X,1,M}+\delta^{(3)}_{X,1,M}\big)(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})+(\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}-\beta^{*})^{\top}I_{X,1,M}(\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}-\beta^{*}),

for some
\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}=\delta_{X,1,M}\hat{\beta}+(1-\delta_{X,1,M})\beta^{*}
for some
\delta_{X,1,M}\in(0,1)
. Here
\delta^{(1)}_{X,1,M}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}),\delta^{(2)}_{X,1,M}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}),\delta^{(3)}_{X,1,M}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})
are the first three terms of (
S81
), and
I_{X,1,M}
is the second order derivative matrix of
\gamma_{1}^{(1)}(X,M)/f_{1}(M\mid X,\beta)
with respect to
\beta
, localized at
\beta=\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}
. In particular, one has

\displaystyle I_{X,1,M}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle F^{\prime\prime}_{1}(M\mid X,\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M})

\displaystyle\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}}k_{u^{*}}(M,m_{0})c_{\rho}^{(1)}\big\{F_{1}(M\mid X,\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}),F_{0}(m_{0}\mid X,\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M})\big\}f_{0}(m_{0}\mid X,\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M})dm_{0}

\displaystyle+F^{\prime}_{1}(M\mid X,\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M})\{F^{\prime}_{1}(M\mid X,\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M})\}^{\top}

\displaystyle\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}}k_{u^{*}}(M,m_{0})c_{\rho}^{(11)}\big\{F_{1}(M\mid X,\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}),F_{0}(m_{0}\mid X,\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M})\big\}f_{0}(m_{0}\mid X,\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M})dm_{0}

\displaystyle+\dots

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}k_{u^{*}}(M,m_{0})c_{\rho}\big\{F_{1}(M\mid X,\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}),F_{0}(m_{0}\mid X,\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M})\big\}f^{\prime\prime}_{0}(m_{0}\mid X,\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M})dm_{0}.

We can define
\delta^{(1)}_{X,0,M}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}),\delta^{(2)}_{X,0,M}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}),\delta^{(3)}_{X,0,M}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})
and
I_{X,0,M}
in a similar manner. We then write,

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,n}^{(2,1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1,2,3}\mathbb{P}_{n}\Big[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\delta_{X,Z,M}^{(j)}\Big](\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})

(S82)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}_{n}\Big[(\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}-\beta^{*})^{\top}\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}I_{X,Z,M}(\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}-\beta^{*})\Big].

For
j=1,2,3
, similar to (
S77
), we can show that uniformly,

\sup_{x,z,m}\|\delta_{x,z,m}^{(j)}\|=\max\Big\{\sup_{x,m}\|\delta_{x,1,m}^{(j)}\|,\,\sup_{x,m}\|\delta_{x,0,m}^{(j)}\|\Big\}\precsim h^{-1}.

To illustrate this, we use
\sup_{x,m}\|\delta_{x,1,m}^{(1)}\|
as an example. In particular, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{x,m}\|\delta_{x,1,m}^{(1)}\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{x,m}\Big[\Big\|F^{\prime}_{1m}(x\mid\beta^{*})\}^{\top}\int_{\mathbb{R}}k_{u^{*}}(m,m_{0})c_{\rho}^{(1)}\big\{F_{1m}(x),F_{0x}(m_{0})\big\}f_{0m_{0}}(x)dm_{0}\Big\|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{x,m}\Big[\Big\|F^{\prime}_{1m}(x\mid\beta^{*})\Big\|\cdot\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}}k_{u^{*}}(m,m_{0})c_{\rho}^{(1)}\big\{F_{1m}(x),F_{0x}(m_{0})\big\}f_{0m_{0}}(x)dm_{0}\Big|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{u,v}|c_{\rho}^{(1)}(u,v)|\cdot\sup_{x,m}|f_{0m}(x)|\cdot\sup_{x,m}\Big[\Big\|F^{\prime}_{1m}(x\mid\beta^{*})\Big\|\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}}k_{u^{*}}(m,m_{0})dm_{0}\Big]

\displaystyle\precsim

\displaystyle h^{-1},

by Assumptions
S9
-
S13
. Then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\delta_{X,Z,M,k}^{(j)}\Big]^{2}\precsim h^{-1}\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\Big]^{2}

(S83)

\displaystyle\precsim

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[{\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}^{2}}\Big]\quad(\text{Assumption \ref{am:pi}})

\displaystyle\precsim

\displaystyle h^{-1}.\quad(\text{Assumption \ref{am:var:Y}}).

Here
\delta_{X,Z,M,k}^{(j)}
represents the
k
th coordinate of
\delta_{X,Z,M}^{(j)}
. Finally by a similar argument as (
S68
)–(
S69
), one can show the following by Chebyshev’s inequality,

\displaystyle(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})\Bigg[\Bigg|\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\delta_{X,Z,M,k}^{(j)}\Bigg|\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2}),

and by an entry-wise argument, we have,

\displaystyle\Bigg\|(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})\Bigg[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\delta_{X,Z,M}^{(j)}\Bigg]\Bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2}).

(S84)

In addition, with an entry-wise argument, we can also show that,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Big\|\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}I_{X,Z,M}\Big]\Big\|_{2}\Bigg]\leq\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{1\leq l_{1},l_{2}\leq d_{\beta}}\Bigg|\Bigg[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}I_{X,Z,M}\Bigg]_{l_{1}l_{2}}\Bigg|\Bigg]\precsim h^{-1},

(S85)

and similarly as (
S83
)–(
S84
) we have

\displaystyle(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})\Bigg[\Bigg\|\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}I_{X,Z,M}\Big]\Bigg\|_{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2}).

Summarizing the results above and by the fact that
\sup_{x,z,m}\|\tilde{\beta}_{x,z,m}-\beta^{*}\|=\sup_{x,z,m}\delta_{x,z,m}\|\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\|\leq\|\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\|=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})
, we have

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,n}^{(2,1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1,2,3}\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\delta_{X,Z,M}^{(j)}\Bigg](\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1,2,3}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})\Bigg[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\delta_{X,Z,M}^{(j)}\Bigg](\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})

\displaystyle+\Big\{(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})+\mathbb{P}\Big\}\Bigg[\|\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}-\beta^{*}\|^{2}\Big(\frac{\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}-\beta^{*}}{\|\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}-\beta^{*}\|}\Big)^{\top}\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\cdot I_{X,Z,M}\Big(\frac{\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}-\beta^{*}}{\|\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}-\beta^{*}\|}\Big)\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\sum_{j=1,2,3}\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\delta_{X,Z,M}^{(j)}\Bigg](\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})}_{\Gamma^{(2,1)}_{2,n}}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2})

where in the last equality we use the property of spectral norm such that by (
S85
),

\displaystyle\Bigg|\Big\{(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})+\mathbb{P}\Big\}\Bigg[\|\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}-\beta^{*}\|^{2}\Big(\frac{\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}-\beta^{*}}{\|\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}-\beta^{*}\|}\Big)^{\top}\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\cdot I_{X,Z,M}\Big(\frac{\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}-\beta^{*}}{\|\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}-\beta^{*}\|}\Big)\Bigg]\Bigg|

(S86)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\|^{2}\Big\{(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})+\mathbb{P}\Big\}\Bigg[\sup_{\|v\|=1}\Bigg|v^{\top}\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}I_{X,Z,M}\Bigg|\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\|^{2}\Big\{(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})+\mathbb{P}\Big\}\Bigg[\Bigg\|\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}I_{X,Z,M}\Bigg\|_{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1}),

and also for
j=1,2,3
, by (
S84
),

\displaystyle\Big\|(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})\Big[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\delta_{X,Z,M}^{(j)}\Big](\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})\Big\|

(S87)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big\|(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})\Big[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\delta_{X,Z,M}^{(j)}\Big]\Big\|_{2}\Big\|\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\Big\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1}).

Next we study
\Delta_{2,n}^{(2,2)}
.
When
\bar{\pi}=\pi
and
\bar{\mu}=\mu
, it is easy to see
\Delta_{2,n}^{(2,2)}=0
. When either
\bar{\pi}\neq\pi
and
\bar{\mu}\neq\mu
, similar to (
S82
), we have

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,n}^{(2,2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1,2,3}\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\Big[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}-\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\Big]\delta_{X,Z,M}^{(j)}\Bigg](\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[(\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}-\beta^{*})^{\top}\Big[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}-\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\Big]I_{X,Z,M}(\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}-\beta^{*})\Bigg].

We then exploit the same technical argument as for
\Delta_{2,n}^{(2,1)}
to bound
\Delta_{2,n}^{(2,2)}
. In particular, recalling
\|\delta^{(j)}_{X,Z,M}\|
can be uniformly bounded by
\mathcal{O}(h^{-1})
, we have for any
j=1,2,3
and
k\in[d_{\beta}]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Big[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}-\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\Big]\delta_{X,Z,M}^{(j)}\Bigg]^{2}

(S88)

\displaystyle\precsim

\displaystyle h^{-2}\cdot\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Big[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}-\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\Big]\Bigg]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2h^{-2}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Big[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\Big]\Bigg]^{2}+2h^{-2}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Big[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\Big]\Bigg]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4h^{-2}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Big[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\Big]\Bigg]^{2}+4h^{-2}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Big[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\Big]\Bigg]^{2}

\displaystyle+2h^{-2}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Big[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\Big]\Bigg]^{2}

\displaystyle\precsim

\displaystyle h^{-2},

where the first and third term in (
S88
) can be bounded similar to (
S83
), while the second term in (
S88
) can be bounded by Assumptions
S8
and
4
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Big[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\Big]\Bigg]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon^{-2}\cdot\big\{\mathbb{E}\big|\mu_{Z}(X,M)\big|^{2}+\mathbb{E}\big|\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big|^{2}\big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(1).

Similar to (
S84
), (
S88
) combining with Chebyshev’s inequality and an entry-wise argument implies that

\displaystyle\Bigg\|(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})\Bigg[\Big[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}-\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\Big]\delta_{X,Z,M}^{(j)}\Bigg]\Bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2}).

Then, a similar argument to (
S83
)–(
S87
) can show that

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,n}^{(2,2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\sum_{j=1,2,3}\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\Big[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}-\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\Big]\delta_{X,Z,M}^{(j)}\Bigg](\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})}_{\Gamma^{(2,2)}_{2,n}}

\displaystyle+o_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2})

Next we bound
\Delta_{2,n}^{(2,3)}
. Based on (
S6
), we can show

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,n}^{(2,3)}

(S89)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\Big[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}-\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\Big]\big(\delta^{(1)}_{X,1,M}+\delta^{(2)}_{X,1,M}+\delta^{(3)}_{X,1,M}\big)\Bigg](\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\Big[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}-\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{Y-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\Big](\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}-\beta^{*})^{\top}I_{X,1,M}(\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}-\beta^{*})\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg\{\big(\kappa^{(1)}_{X,Z,Y}+\kappa^{(2)}_{X,Z,M}\big)\big(\delta^{(1)}_{X,1,M}+\delta^{(2)}_{X,1,M}+\delta^{(3)}_{X,1,M}\big)\Bigg\}(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg\{\big(\kappa^{(1)}_{X,Z,Y}+\kappa^{(2)}_{X,Z,M}\big)(\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}-\beta^{*})^{\top}I_{X,1,M}(\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}-\beta^{*})\Bigg\},

where we further define,

\displaystyle\kappa_{X,Z,Y}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X)}Y-\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)}Y,

\displaystyle\kappa_{X,Z,M}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)-\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M).

We argue that all terms in (
S89
) are
o_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2})
and thus

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,n}^{(2,3)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2}),

(S90)

is negligible. For example, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{P}_{n}\big(\kappa_{X,Z,Y}^{(1)}\delta_{X,1,M}^{(1)}\big)(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\|\cdot\mathbb{P}_{n}\big(\big\|\kappa_{X,Z,Y}^{(1)}\delta_{X,1,M}^{(1)}\big\|\big)

(S91)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2}),

where
\|\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\|=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})
and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\big(\big\|\kappa_{X,Z,Y}^{(1)}\delta_{X,1,M}^{(1)}\big\|\big)

(S92)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\Big\|\Big\{\frac{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)-\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X)}{\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X)\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\Big\}YF^{\prime}_{1M}(X\mid\beta^{*})\int_{\mathbb{R}}k_{u^{*}}(M,m_{0})c_{\rho}^{(1)}\big\{F_{1M}(X),F_{0X}(m_{0})\big\}f_{0m_{0}}(X)dm_{0}\Big\|\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)-\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X)\|_{\infty}\|\hat{\pi}^{-1}_{Z}(X)\|_{\infty}\|\bar{\pi}_{Z}^{-1}(X)\|_{\infty}\cdot\sup_{m_{1},x}\|F^{\prime}_{1m_{1}}(x\mid\beta^{*})\|\cdot\sup_{u,v}|c_{\rho}^{(1)}(u,v)|\cdot\sup_{m_{0},x}f_{0m_{0}}(x)

\displaystyle\cdot\Big\{\sup_{m_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}k_{u^{*}}(u)dm_{0}\Big\}\mathbb{P}_{n}(|Y|)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)\cdot\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)\cdots\mathcal{O}(1)\cdot\mathcal{O}(h^{-1})\cdot\{(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{E})(|Y|)+\mathbb{E}(|Y|)\}

(S93)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}),

where
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}
denotes the sup-norm for function and random variable. For (
S92
), we have
\|\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)-\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X)\|_{\infty}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
under Assumption
4
,
\|\bar{\pi}^{-1}_{Z}(X)\|_{\infty}\geq\epsilon^{-1}
under Assumption
S8
and thus
\|\hat{\pi}^{-1}_{Z}(X)\|_{\infty}
is also bounded away from zero with probability approaching
1
, meanwhile all other terms are
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
under Assumptions
S9
–
S13
. For (
S93
),
\sup_{m_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}k_{u^{*}}(u)dm_{0}=\mathcal{O}(h^{-1})
under Assumption
S9
,
\mathbb{E}(|Y|)\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(|Y|^{2})}<+\infty
under Assumption
S12
and then
(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{E})(|Y|)=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})
follows by an application of the Markov’s inequality. Similarly, we can show

\big|\mathbb{P}_{n}\big(\kappa_{X,Z,Y}^{(1)}\delta_{X,1,M}^{(2)}\big)(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})\big|=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2})\quad\text{and}\quad\big|\mathbb{P}_{n}\big(\kappa_{X,Z,Y}^{(1)}\delta_{X,1,M}^{(3)}\big)(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})\big|=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2}).

We can also show that

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{P}_{n}\big(\kappa_{X,Z,M}^{(2)}\delta_{X,1,M}^{(1)}\big)(\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\|\mathbb{P}_{n}\big(\big\|\kappa_{X,Z,Y}^{(2)}\delta_{X,1,M}^{(1)}\big\|\big)

(S94)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2}),

where
\|\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*}\|=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-1/2})
, and similar to (
S91
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\big(\big\|\kappa_{X,Z,Y}^{(2)}\delta_{X,1,M}^{(1)}\big\|\big)

(S95)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg[\Bigg\|\Bigg[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)-\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\Bigg]F^{\prime}_{1M}(X\mid\beta^{*})\int_{\mathbb{R}}k_{u^{*}}(M,m_{0})

\displaystyle\cdot c_{\rho}^{(1)}\big\{F_{1M}(X),F_{0X}(m_{0})\big\}f_{0m_{0}}(X)dm_{0}\Bigg\|\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Bigg\|\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)-\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}+\Bigg\{\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)}-\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\Bigg\}\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\Bigg\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\cdot\sup_{m_{1},x}\|F^{\prime}_{1m_{1}}(x\mid\beta^{*})\|\cdot\sup_{u,v}|c_{\rho}^{(1)}(u,v)|\cdot\sup_{m_{0},x}f_{0m_{i}}(x)\cdot\sup_{m_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}k_{u^{*}}(u)dm_{0}\cdot\mathbb{P}_{n}(|Y|)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}).

For (
S95
), based on Assumptions
S8
and
4
, we bound

\displaystyle\Bigg\|\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)-\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}+\Bigg\{\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)}-\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\Bigg\}\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\Bigg\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Bigg\|\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\big\{\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)-\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\Bigg\|_{\infty}+\Bigg\|\Bigg\{\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)}-\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}}{\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\Bigg\}\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\Bigg\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon^{-1}\|\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)-\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\|_{\infty}+\Big\|\frac{\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X)-\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)}{\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X)}\Big\|_{\infty}\|\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon^{-1}\|\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)-\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\|_{\infty}+\|\bar{\pi}_{Z}(X)-\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X)\|_{\infty}\|\hat{\pi}^{-1}_{Z}(X)\|_{\infty}\|\bar{\pi}_{Z}^{-1}(X)\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\cdot\Big\{\|\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\|_{\infty}+\|\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)-\bar{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\|_{\infty}\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

By applying the similar technical arguments as above to each entry of
\mathbb{P}_{n}(\kappa^{(1)}_{X,Z,Y}I_{X,1,M})
and
\mathbb{P}_{n}(\kappa^{(2)}_{X,Z,M}I_{X,1,M})
, we can show that
\mathbb{P}_{n}(\|\kappa^{(1)}_{X,Z,Y}I_{X,1,M}\|_{2}),\mathbb{P}_{n}(\|\kappa^{(2)}_{X,Z,M}I_{X,1,M}\|_{2})=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1})
similar to (
S85
). Then by the basic property of spectral norm and similar to (
S86
), one has

\displaystyle\Bigg|\mathbb{P}_{n}\Bigg\{\big(\kappa^{(1)}_{X,Z,Y}+\kappa^{(2)}_{X,Z,M}\big)(\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}-\beta^{*})^{\top}I_{X,1,M}(\tilde{\beta}_{X,1,M}-\beta^{*})\Bigg\}\Bigg|

(S96)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\hat{\beta}-\beta\|^{2}\Big\{\mathbb{P}_{n}\big(\|\kappa^{(1)}_{X,Z,Y}I_{X,1,M}\|_{2}\big)+\mathbb{P}_{n}\big(\|\kappa^{(2)}_{X,Z,M}I_{X,1,M}\|_{2}\big)\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1}).

Combining (
S89
)–(
S96
), (
S90
) is thus shown.

Summarizing the results above, we have

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,n}=\Gamma^{(1)}_{2,n}+\Gamma^{(2,1)}_{2,n}+\Gamma^{(2,2)}_{2,n}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2})+\mathbb{P}_{n}\{h(V\mid\hat{\pi},\hat{\mu})-h(V\mid\bar{\pi},\bar{\mu})\}

(S97)

We now study the last term on the right-hand side above. We decompose

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\{h(V\mid\hat{\pi},\hat{\mu})-h(V\mid\bar{\pi},\bar{\mu})\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})\{h(V\mid\hat{\pi},\hat{\mu})-h(V\mid\bar{\pi},\bar{\mu})\}+\mathbb{P}\{h(V\mid\hat{\pi},\hat{\mu})-h(V\mid\bar{\pi},\bar{\mu})\}.

We first bound
(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})\{h(V\mid\hat{\pi},\hat{\mu})-h(V\mid\bar{\pi},\bar{\mu})\}
through Lemma
S4
using empirical process theory. We defer a brief introduction of empirical process theory as well as the technical proof of Lemma
S4
to Section
S9
.

Lemma S4

Recall (
). Under the conditions of Theorem
5
, we have

\displaystyle(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P})\{h(V\mid\hat{\pi},\hat{\mu})-h(V\mid\bar{\pi},\bar{\mu})\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2}).

(S98)

By Lemma
S4
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}\big\{h(V\mid\hat{\pi},\hat{\mu})-h(V\mid\bar{\pi},\bar{\mu})\big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2})+\mathbb{P}\big\{h(V\mid\hat{\pi},\hat{\mu})-h(V\mid\bar{\pi},\bar{\mu})\big\}.

In addition, if either
\bar{\mu}=\mu
or
\bar{\pi}=\pi
, one has

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\{h(V\mid\bar{\pi},\bar{\mu})\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bar{{\xi}}_{1}(X,Z,M)\{Y-\mu_{Z}(X,M)\}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\bar{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\bar{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u){e}_{u}(X)du\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{\tau}_{u^{*}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(X)du\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tau_{u^{*}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(X)du\Big\}\quad\text{(Theorem \ref{thm:dbrb})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\mu_{1}(X,m_{1})-\mu_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(X)du\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[{{\xi}}_{1}(X,Z,M)\{Y-\mu_{Z}(X,M)\}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big\{\mu_{1}(X,m_{1})-\mu_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}k_{u^{*}}(u)e_{u}(X)du\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big\{h(V\mid\pi,\mu)\big\},

where the fourth equality is by Theorem
2
, the fifth equality is by
\mathbb{E}\tilde{\xi}_{1}=0
when
\tilde{\mu}=\mu
; See (
S66
). Therefore we have,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big\{h(V\mid\hat{\pi},\hat{\mu})-h(V\mid\bar{\pi},\bar{\mu})\big\}

(S99)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big\{h(V\mid\hat{\pi},\hat{\mu})-h(V\mid{\pi},{\mu})\big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}{\gamma}_{1}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X){f}_{Z}(M\mid X,{\beta}^{*})}\big\{Y-\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\{\hat{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\hat{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\}e_{u}(X)du

\displaystyle-\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}{\gamma}_{1}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{{\pi}_{Z}(X){f}_{Z}(M\mid X,{\beta}^{*})}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\{\mu_{1}(X,m_{1})-\mu_{0}(X,m_{0})\}e_{u}(X)du\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}{\gamma}_{1}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X){f}_{Z}(M\mid X,{\beta}^{*})}\big\{Y-\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}-\frac{(-1)^{Z+1}{\gamma}_{1}^{(Z)}(X,M)}{{\pi}_{Z}(X){f}_{Z}(M\mid X,{\beta}^{*})}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\big\}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\{\hat{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\hat{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\}e_{u}(X)du-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\{{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\}e_{u}(X)du\Bigg].

By LOTE, the first term in (
) equals to,

\displaystyle\sum_{z=0,1}\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\mathbb{P}\Big[{\pi}_{z}(X)\frac{(-1)^{z+1}{\gamma}_{1}^{(z)}(X,M)}{\hat{\pi}_{z}(X){f}_{z}(M\mid X,{\beta}^{*})}\big\{Y-\hat{\mu}_{z}(X,M)\big\}

\displaystyle-\frac{(-1)^{z+1}{\gamma}_{1}^{(z)}(X,M)}{{f}_{z}(M\mid X,{\beta}^{*})}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{z}(X,M)\big\}\mid X,Z=z\Big]\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{z=0,1}\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\mathbb{P}\Big[{\pi}_{z}(X)\frac{(-1)^{z+1}{\gamma}_{1}^{(z)}(X,M)}{\hat{\pi}_{z}(X){f}_{z}(M\mid X,{\beta}^{*})}\big\{Y-\hat{\mu}_{z}(X,M)\big\}

\displaystyle-\frac{(-1)^{z+1}{\gamma}_{1}^{(z)}(X,M)}{{f}_{z}(M\mid X,{\beta}^{*})}\big\{Y-{\mu}_{z}(X,M)\big\}\mid X,Z=z,M\Big]\mid X,Z=z\Bigg]\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{z=0,1}\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\mathbb{P}\Bigg[{\pi}_{z}(X)\frac{(-1)^{z+1}{\gamma}_{1}^{(z)}(X,M)}{\hat{\pi}_{z}(X){f}_{z}(M\mid X,{\beta}^{*})}\big\{\mu_{z}(X,M)-\hat{\mu}_{z}(X,M)\big\}

\displaystyle-\frac{(-1)^{z+1}{\gamma}_{1}^{(z)}(X,M)}{{f}_{z}(M\mid X,{\beta}^{*})}\big\{\underbrace{\mu_{z}(X,M)-{\mu}_{z}(X,M)}_{=0}\big\}\mid X,Z=z\Bigg]\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{z=0,1}\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\frac{{\pi}_{z}(X)(-1)^{z+1}}{\hat{\pi}_{z}(X)}\mathbb{P}\Big[\frac{{\gamma}_{1}^{(z)}(X,M)}{{f}_{z}(M\mid X,{\beta}^{*})}\big\{\mu_{z}(X,M)-\hat{\mu}_{z}(X,M)\big\}\mid X,Z=z\Big]\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{z=0,1}\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\frac{{\pi}_{z}(X)(-1)^{z+1}}{\hat{\pi}_{z}(X)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\{\mu_{z}(X,m_{z})-\hat{\mu}_{z}(X,m_{z})\}e_{u}(X)du\Bigg],

which combining with (
S99
) yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\{h(V\mid\hat{\pi},\hat{\mu})-h(V\mid\bar{\pi},\bar{\mu})\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{z=0,1}(-1)^{z+1}\cdot\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\Big\{\frac{{\pi}_{z}(X)}{\hat{\pi}_{z}(X)}-1\Big\}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\{\mu_{z}(X,m_{z})-\hat{\mu}_{z}(X,m_{z})\}e_{u}(X)du\Bigg].

For either
z=0\text{ or 1}
, by Cauchy–Schwartz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\Bigg|\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\Big\{\frac{{\pi}_{z}(X)}{\hat{\pi}_{z}(X)}-1\Big\}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\{\mu_{z}(X,m_{z})-\hat{\mu}_{z}(X,m_{z})\}e_{u}(X)du\Bigg]\Bigg|

(S101)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Bigg|\mathbb{P}\Bigg\{\frac{{\pi}_{z}(X)-\hat{\pi}_{z}(X)}{\hat{\pi}_{z}(X)}\Bigg\}^{2}\Bigg|^{1/2}\cdot\sqrt{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k^{2}_{u^{*}}(u)du}\cdot\Bigg|\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\{\mu_{z}(X,m_{z})-\hat{\mu}_{z}(X,m_{z})\}^{2}e^{2}_{u}(X)du\Bigg]\Bigg|^{1/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}r_{\pi}r_{\mu}).

To see (
S101
), firstly we note by Assumptions
S8
and
4
and LOTE,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big[\{{\pi}_{z}(X)-\hat{\pi}_{z}(X)\}^{2}\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big[\mathbb{P}\big[\{{\pi}_{Z}(X)-\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X)\}^{2}\mid X,Z=z\big]\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\pi_{z}(X)^{-1}\cdot\mathbb{P}\Big[\pi_{z}(X)\mathbb{P}\big[\{{\pi}_{Z}(X)-\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X)\}^{2}\mid X,Z=z\big]

\displaystyle+\pi_{1-z}(X)\mathbb{P}\big[\{{\pi}_{Z}(X)-\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X)\}^{2}\mid X,Z=1-z\big]\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{z}(X)^{-1}\cdot\mathbb{P}\Big[\mathbb{P}\big[\{{\pi}_{Z}(X)-\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X)\}^{2}\mid X\big]\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{z}(X)^{-1}\cdot\mathbb{P}\big[\{{\pi}_{Z}(X)-\hat{\pi}_{Z}(X)\}^{2}\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(r_{\pi}^{2}).

and thus

\displaystyle\Bigg|\mathbb{P}\Bigg\{\frac{{\pi}_{z}(X)-\hat{\pi}_{z}(X)}{\hat{\pi}_{z}(X)}\Bigg\}^{2}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\hat{\pi}^{-1}\|_{\infty}^{2}\cdot\big|\mathbb{P}\{{\pi}_{z}(X)-\hat{\pi}_{z}(X)\}^{2}\big|=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(r_{\pi}^{2}),

because
\|\hat{\pi}^{-1}\|_{\infty}\precsim 1
with probability approaching
1
under Assumptions
S8
and
4
, where
\bar{\pi}_{z}(X)\precsim 1
and
\|\hat{\pi}-\bar{\pi}\|_{\infty}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Secondly, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k^{2}_{u^{*}}(u)du\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\{\mu_{z}(X,m_{z})-\hat{\mu}_{z}(X,m_{z})\}^{2}e^{2}_{u}(X)du\Bigg]

\displaystyle\precsim

\displaystyle h^{-2}\cdot\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\{\mu_{z}(X,m_{z})-\hat{\mu}_{z}(X,m_{z})\}^{2}e^{2}_{u}(X)du\Bigg]\quad\text{(Assumption \ref{am:kernel})}

\displaystyle\precsim

\displaystyle h^{-2}\cdot\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\{\mu_{z}(X,m_{z})-\hat{\mu}_{z}(X,m_{z})\}^{2}e_{u}(X)du\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h^{-2}\cdot\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\mathbb{P}\Big[\{\mu_{z}(X,M_{z})-\hat{\mu}_{z}(X,M_{z})\}^{2}\mid X\Big]\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h^{-2}\cdot\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\mathbb{P}\Big[\{\mu_{Z}(X,M)-\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\}^{2}\mid X,Z=z\Big]\Bigg]\quad(\text{Assumption \ref{assump:TAignorability}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle h^{-2}\cdot\pi_{z}^{-1}(X)\cdot\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\pi_{z}(X)\mathbb{P}\Big[\{\mu_{Z}(X,M)-\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\}^{2}\mid X,Z=z\Big]

\displaystyle+\pi_{1-z}(X)\mathbb{P}\Big[\{\mu_{Z}(X,M)-\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\}^{2}\mid X,Z=1-z\Big]\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h^{-2}\cdot\pi_{z}^{-1}(X)\cdot\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\mathbb{P}\Big[\{\mu_{Z}(X,M)-\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\}^{2}\mid X\Big]\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h^{-2}\cdot\pi_{z}^{-1}(X)\cdot\mathbb{P}\Big[\{\mu_{Z}(X,M)-\hat{\mu}_{Z}(X,M)\}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(h^{-2}r_{\mu}^{2})\quad\text{(Assumptions \ref{am:pi} and \ref{am:emp})},

where the second inequality is by
e_{u}(x)
is uniformly bounded by some constants for all
x\in\mathbb{X}
and
u\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, under Assumptions
S10
and
S13
. Summarizing results above, (
S101
) is thus shown.
We finally conclude,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\{h(V\mid\hat{\pi},\hat{\mu})-h(V\mid\bar{\pi},\bar{\mu})\}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}r_{\pi}r_{\mu})

\displaystyle\text{and}\quad\mathbb{P}_{n}\{h(V\mid\hat{\pi},\hat{\mu})-h(V\mid\bar{\pi},\bar{\mu})\}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}r_{\pi}r_{\mu})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2}),

which combining with (
S97
) yields

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma_{2,n}^{(1)}+\Gamma_{2,n}^{(2,1)}+\Gamma_{2,n}^{(2,2)}+\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}r_{\pi}r_{\mu})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2})

(S102)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\Gamma_{2,n}}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2})}+\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}r_{\pi}r_{\mu})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(h^{-1}n^{-1/2}),

where in summary, we define,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{2,n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{z=0,1}\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\big\{\bar{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\bar{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}c_{\rho}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\beta^{*}),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\big\}

\displaystyle f_{(1-z)m_{1-z}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\{f^{\prime}_{zm_{z}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\}^{\top}du\Bigg](\hat{\beta}-\beta^{*})

\displaystyle+\sum_{z=0,1}\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}k_{u^{*}}(u)\big\{\bar{\mu}_{1}(X,m_{1})-\bar{\mu}_{0}(X,m_{0})\big\}c_{\rho}^{(z)}\big\{{F}_{1m_{1}}(X\mid\beta^{*}),{F}_{0m_{0}}(X\mid\beta^{*})\big\}
